# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-03

Today adds 34 newly screened tools and completes 12 earlier screened entries: 45 detailed guides plus one pending-profile lead, with no fixed count quota. The expanded plan covers 83 fields, 687 repository queries and 18 model tasks, adding anamorphic art and glasses-free display workflows. Current README, complete software-license and supplementary evidence distinguish actual AI work from procedural utilities, thin scaffolds and duplicate mirrors. Guides cover accessible video description, creative coding, neural audio, light-field capture, animation, CAD, comic cleanup and fabrication. Name It misses its own held-out reliability targets; FourStem’s weight grant remains unresolved; OBSIDIAN’s binary component terms are not fully established; Contsy’s simulated VRAM caps are distinguished from tested hardware. None of these observations grants a recommendation. Palmier Pro’s current proprietary download route is separated from historical GPL source. Yesterday’s complete 1,512-tool weekly review is preserved; today’s new records receive fresh six-check assessments, with current-library weekly coverage verified separately. All platforms are represented through documented support rather than inferred compatibility. Every guide is a documentation review: no discovered app was installed or run. Raw unreviewed and excluded candidates stay local, and earlier pending guides remain queued. Verified collection: 687/687 repository queries attempted; 684 successful, 1 partial, 2 failed and 122 bounded/truncated. All 18 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 27 watchlist fetches and 2 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

| Learned anamorphoses and distorted-view art | LookingGlass’s primary paper describes diffusion-guided generative anamorphosis for a mirror viewpoint; the initially proposed project URL returned 404, so the paper was fetched separately. The already indexed Latent Generative Anamorphoses project is retained without recycling an unchanged guide. Conventional projection mapping alone does not establish AI relevance. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08902) [Source 2](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses) |
| Neural multiview and glasses-free display art | The primary light-field neural-rendering project describes transformer-based view synthesis with epipolar/reference aggregation. altiro3D’s documented MiDaS-to-quilt workflow targets Looking Glass Portrait; neural view synthesis does not itself establish compatibility with every glasses-free display. [Source 1](https://light-field-neural-rendering.github.io/) [Source 2](https://github.com/canessae/altiro3D) |

## Search and review counts

83 creative fields; 687/687 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 29018 distinct source candidates and 863 model leads. 45 detailed profiles and 1 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 2 failed repository queries, 1 partial repository queries, 122 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 2 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Live primary README captures were read for Embedded Amp Model, AudioStellar and LibreTerrain. The amp project distinguishes synthetic tests from a future physical pedal; AudioStellar documents JUCE/CMake with macOS instructions still ToDo; LibreTerrain distinguishes historical Mac downloads from disabled current Mac CI. These current observations are context, not a newly cleared grant or repeated guide. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ar39907/embedded-amp-model/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ayrsd/audiostellar/-/raw/audiostellar2/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/libreterrain/libreterrain/-/raw/main/README.md)
- **codeberg** · gap: The live web discovery attempt was denied by robots restrictions. No usable current Codeberg source was read through that route and no access restriction was bypassed. This is a source-group gap, not proof that no creative AI projects exist.
- **sourcehut** · gap: The live SourceHut discovery/read attempt was denied by robots restrictions. Yesterday’s Diffuser source review is preserved but is not misrepresented as today’s source access. No newly qualified SourceHut tool was established.
- **packages** · searched: The current ruah-mcp npm metadata was captured and compared with its source documentation. Node 20+, platform-specific WebGPU/CPU routing and separate model downloads are documented; package presence does not prove independent reproduction or every weight license. [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/ruah-mcp/latest) [Source](https://github.com/0-big/ruah-mcp)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Current source reviews cover the MOSS ComfyUI editing nodes, Blender MCP workflows, OBSIDIAN-Neural and creative-agent skills. The Comfy Registry homepage returned a limited application shell, so no exhaustive registry coverage is claimed. Models, DAWs, proprietary CAD hosts and paid services remain distinct from wrapper licenses. Separately reviewed MOSS Edit and Base+SFT Merge Presets document native integration, quantization and effect limits; their code grants do not relicense weights. [Source](https://github.com/bag3labo/MOSS-Image-unGPT) [Source](https://registry.comfy.org/) [Source](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj) [Source](https://github.com/Chenkin-x/ComfyUI-Moss-Image-Edit) [Source](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh primary-source attempts supplement all 83 observed fields through 75 unique web targets: 72 HTTP successes and three failures. Some successful bodies are bounded PDFs or limited shells, not qualified tools. A failed guessed LookingGlass project URL was supplemented by its actual primary paper; OpenSlides SSL and the museum URL remain explicit gaps. [Source](https://somacad.app/) [Source](https://opencadirrigation.com/) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08902) [Source](https://light-field-neural-rendering.github.io/)
- **research-code** · searched: KPI-NeRF and ViSOR now have source/license-backed guides scoped to their actual light-field/optical hardware, environment instructions and released examples. The current LookingGlass paper broadens anamorphic-art discovery without treating paper results as a ready software application or independently reproduced quality. Looking Ahead has released 1.3B/5B inference paths, whereas L3G-World currently lists its inference/checkpoint releases as forthcoming and is held outside eligible additions. [Source](https://github.com/erqihuang/KPI-NeRF) [Source](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08902) [Source](https://github.com/TencentARC/Looking-Ahead) [Source](https://github.com/cvlab-kaist/L3G-World)
- **international** · searched: Current Chinese, Thai and Turkish creative-workflow documentation was reviewed alongside English-language sources, including MOSSunGPT, nuea House, comicDPI and vitrin. Source language and research origin do not determine quality tier; platform, model, commercial and proprietary-host limits remain explicit. [Source](https://github.com/bag3labo/MOSS-Image-unGPT) [Source](https://github.com/HaNorthRich/nuea-ai-blender-house-skill) [Source](https://github.com/yekvisual/vitrin)

## Name It

Accessible media & assistive creation · Video, animation & film · Creative learning & authoring

The repository was created on 2026-11-02. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Drafting English how-to audio descriptions with evidence frames and an explicit volunteer approval queue.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-03. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · failed:** The owner-held-out evaluation misses the stated confident-error and ready-item precision targets: 22 percent confident-and-wrong versus a 10 percent target, and 78 percent ready precision versus 85 percent. Human review remains necessary; this is not independent acceptance. [Source](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Node 24, pnpm and FFmpeg. Install project dependencies, configure the Meta Model API in a local environment file, add a video, run the pipeline and export its bundle into the Next.js app. ElevenLabs voice rendering is optional; the browser voice is a fallback. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers project code. Meta hosted-model access, ElevenLabs and video/media rights are separate. This is not an entirely local inference stack. Hosted Meta processing and optional ElevenLabs charges. The owner reports roughly 11–14 cents per found reference as an illustrative measured cost, not a current provider price guarantee. [Source](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. English how-to videos only; long speech without pauses forces playback interruption. Evaluation uses a model judge and a small owner-labelled test set. Confident mistakes remain too frequent for unattended accessibility delivery.

### How it uses AI

A review-assisted video describer uses Muse transcription and visual reasoning with SAM 3.1 grounding to identify the objects behind spoken references such as this, then places short audio descriptions in pauses. [Source](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A review-assisted video describer uses Muse transcription and visual reasoning with SAM 3.1 grounding to identify the objects behind spoken references such as this, then places short audio descriptions in pauses. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Drafting English how-to audio descriptions with evidence frames and an explicit volunteer approval queue. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README links a live demo, review screenshots and a seven-video owner evaluation. On 37 held-out references it reports 75 percent correct naming, but 22 percent confident-and-wrong and 78 percent accuracy among ready items, missing its own reliability targets. No blind or low-vision user testing is reported. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Node 24, pnpm and FFmpeg. Install project dependencies, configure the Meta Model API in a local environment file, add a video, run the pipeline and export its bundle into the Next.js app. ElevenLabs voice rendering is optional; the browser voice is a fallback. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Node 24, pnpm and FFmpeg. Install project dependencies, configure the Meta Model API in a local environment file, add a video, run the pipeline and export its bundle into the Next.js app. ElevenLabs voice rendering is optional; the browser voice is a fallback.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm add-video my-video.mp4 my-video --domain cooking
```


```sh
pnpm run-pipeline my-video
```


```sh
pnpm export-demo my-video
```


```sh
pnpm dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

1. Add a short English instructional video with visible pointing and a transcript context.
2. Inspect each grounded object and confidence in the review queue; approve, edit or reject the text.
3. Listen to description timing in the accessible player and revise wrong or overlapping cues before sharing.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node 24, pnpm, FFmpeg; Next.js 16/React 19. Meta Model API access for Muse Spark 1.3, SAM 3.1 and Voice Transcribe; optional ElevenLabs.
- **Platforms:** Local Node/browser workflow; tested host OS and minimum browser versions are not documented

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers project code. Meta hosted-model access, ElevenLabs and video/media rights are separate. This is not an entirely local inference stack.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers project code. Meta hosted-model access, ElevenLabs and video/media rights are separate. This is not an entirely local inference stack.
- **Cost:** Hosted Meta processing and optional ElevenLabs charges. The owner reports roughly 11–14 cents per found reference as an illustrative measured cost, not a current provider price guarantee.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

English how-to videos only; long speech without pauses forces playback interruption. Evaluation uses a model judge and a small owner-labelled test set. Confident mistakes remain too frequent for unattended accessibility delivery. [Source 1](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/nathanlegit/NameIt/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This is tile extrusion, not a general image-to-3D neural model. Canvas dimensions, depth and palette constraints apply; previews are not independent topology, printability or game-readiness validation.

### How it uses AI

An MCP server lets an AI assistant draw colored depth tiles, inspect software-rendered previews and build low-poly meshes for a separately purchased Kenney Shape installation. [Source](https://github.com/CuteTechGuy/kenshape-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An MCP server lets an AI assistant draw colored depth tiles, inspect software-rendered previews and build low-poly meshes for a separately purchased Kenney Shape installation. [Source 1](https://github.com/CuteTechGuy/kenshape-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Agent-assisted low-poly props, with editable .kenshape projects and GLB, OBJ, SVG or PNG exports. [Source 1](https://github.com/CuteTechGuy/kenshape-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

A rocket example shows the flat canvas plus three mesh views. A smoke-test description covers export/reload, but no independent asset-quality or game acceptance was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/CuteTechGuy/kenshape-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Clone the repository and run npm install with Node 18+. Supply KENSHAPE_EXE pointing at your own Kenney Shape executable and adjacent data folder, then add src/index.js as a stdio server in an MCP client. [Source 1](https://github.com/CuteTechGuy/kenshape-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the repository and run npm install with Node 18+. Supply KENSHAPE_EXE pointing at your own Kenney Shape executable and adjacent data folder, then add src/index.js as a stdio server in an MCP client.

```sh
npm install
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/CuteTechGuy/kenshape-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

1. Create a small 16- or 24-tile project and ask the assistant for a simple prop.
2. Draw and inspect the preview sheet, correcting tile shape and depth.
3. Export GLB or save .kenshape, then inspect the result in the intended editor.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Video export depends on browser codecs and recording support. ### How it uses AI

A static poster editor asks a chosen language model to write self-contained Canvas 2D illustration/animation code, combines it with editable text and layers, and exports images, HTML, WebM or project JSON. [Source](https://github.com/kaixuanjun/plaque-studio/blob/main/README.md)

The repository was created on 2026-11-02. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Scope: Exploring stem-based performance gestures and auditioning separations locally in a browser.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/LICENSE)

[Source](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Node 22.12+ and run npm ci followed by npm start, then open localhost port 8791. Direct file opening cannot start the required audio workers. First separation downloads a roughly 172 MB model. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** MIT covers project source. The downloaded model repository states no license, so weight permission remains unresolved and is not supplied by the MIT grant. Imported music rights are separate. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. The unresolved rights above are evidence gaps, not a verified dependency clearance. [Source](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Some device controls are best guesses. WebKit storage may last only one session, streaming/online sessions are absent, and a four-minute stem pack is reported around 340 MB while playing, about double in reverse. Model-license uncertainty blocks a recommendation.

### How it uses AI

A procedural 3D browser player separates uploaded music with a downloaded Demucs model, then offers four-stem mixing, looping, effects and recordings through Web Audio. [Source](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A procedural 3D browser player separates uploaded music with a downloaded Demucs model, then offers four-stem mixing, looping, effects and recordings through Web Audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Exploring stem-based performance gestures and auditioning separations locally in a browser. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README includes interaction animations and owner split timings on one Apple Silicon machine. The code-synthesized demo tracks are not neural output evaluations, and the live Pages deployment had not been tested by the author. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Node 22.12+ and run npm ci followed by npm start, then open localhost port 8791. Direct file opening cannot start the required audio workers. First separation downloads a roughly 172 MB model. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Node 22.12+ and run npm ci followed by npm start, then open localhost port 8791. Direct file opening cannot start the required audio workers. First separation downloads a roughly 172 MB model.

```sh
npm ci
```


```sh
npm start
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start with the synthesized demo to learn the transport and stem controls.
2. Load a short audio file you may process, separate it and listen for leakage/artifacts.
3. Set a loop, change stem levels and make a short recording; check saved-library behavior in your browser.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** First split downloads about 172 MB. A four-minute stem pack uses about 340 MB playback memory, approximately doubled in reverse; these are examples, not complete RAM/GPU minima.
- **Software:** Node 22.12+, Web Audio browser and optional WebGPU acceleration. CPU separation is slower. Model weights are cached in the browser; private-file-system support varies.
- **Platforms:** macOS launcher documented; Windows/Linux npm startup documented; Browser/WebGPU support and storage behavior vary; not a hardware-device emulator certification

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** MIT covers project source. The downloaded model repository states no license, so weight permission remains unresolved and is not supplied by the MIT grant. Imported music rights are separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers project source. The downloaded model repository states no license, so weight permission remains unresolved and is not supplied by the MIT grant. Imported music rights are separate.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Some device controls are best guesses. WebKit storage may last only one session, streaming/online sessions are absent, and a four-minute stem pack is reported around 340 MB while playing, about double in reverse. Model-license uncertainty blocks a recommendation. [Source 1](https://github.com/Gitguy268/stem-player/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A learned radiance-field and spectral-subspace implementation reconstructs view- and wavelength-dependent imagery from a single kaleidoscopic plenoptic snapshot, including up to 29 spectral channels. [Source](https://github.com/erqihuang/KPI-NeRF/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A learned radiance-field and spectral-subspace implementation reconstructs view- and wavelength-dependent imagery from a single kaleidoscopic plenoptic snapshot, including up to 29 spectral channels. [Source 1](https://github.com/erqihuang/KPI-NeRF/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README includes an EPI preview and dataset link. Public quantitative results and matching reproducible checkpoints are still incomplete; paper venue and preview imagery do not establish an installation or reconstruction-quality pass. Use Linux and an NVIDIA CUDA-compatible PyTorch environment. The README specifies Python 3.8 and a Conda environment file. Download the separately published Zenodo scenes, set scene/calibration paths, then use the Ajar training/evaluation script or a matching Ball checkpoint. [Source 1](https://github.com/erqihuang/KPI-NeRF/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.

```sh
conda env create -f environment.yml
```


```sh
conda activate kpi-nerf
```


```sh
bash config/ajar_kpi-nerf.sh
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/erqihuang/KPI-NeRF/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A C++ conversion library uses MiDaS depth to turn a flat image/video into multiview light-field output for a calibrated slanted lenticular display such as Looking Glass Portrait. [Source 1](https://github.com/canessae/altiro3D/blob/main/README.md)

An edit LoRA re-samples a MOSS-Image SFT output in ComfyUI toward flatter hand-drawn anime shading, while conditioning on the original composition. [Source](https://github.com/bag3labo/moss-image-ungpt/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An edit LoRA re-samples a MOSS-Image SFT output in ComfyUI toward flatter hand-drawn anime shading, while conditioning on the original composition. [Source 1](https://github.com/bag3labo/moss-image-ungpt/blob/main/README.md)

Update ComfyUI to include Text Encode MOSS-Image Edit. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Upscaling can hallucinate text. The iReader profile substitutes a comparable Kobo profile rather than native device support. Missing pages use originals, cancellation waits at file boundaries, and a packaged Windows folder does not establish clean-machine acceptance.

### How it uses AI

A Windows pipeline unpacks comic pages, enlarges them with Real-ESRGAN through spandrel, applies Kindle Comic Converter e-ink layout and creates a CBZ while recording failed pages. [Source](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/SETUP_FOR_AI.md)

### Install

Use a dedicated Windows Python 3.13 environment and NVIDIA CUDA GPU. Install the matching PyTorch/torchvision CUDA pair before project requirements, download the selected RealESRGAN model to models, then start the CLI or PySide6 desktop app. The setup note names torch 2.6.0 and torchvision 0.21.0; compatibility still needs checking in the chosen environment. [Source 1](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/SETUP_FOR_AI.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use a dedicated Windows Python 3.13 environment and NVIDIA CUDA GPU. Install the matching PyTorch/torchvision CUDA pair before project requirements, download the selected RealESRGAN model to models, then start the CLI or PySide6 desktop app. The setup note names torch 2.6.0 and torchvision 0.21.0; compatibility still needs checking in the chosen environment.

```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install torch torchvision --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu124
```


```sh
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m comicdpi.desktop.app
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/SETUP_FOR_AI.md)

1. Choose one short source comic and the correct device preset/model.
2. Compare linework and small lettering; inspect the failed-page report and original-page fallbacks.
3. Export a CBZ and inspect it on the intended e-reader before processing a larger library.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/yoyoset/comicdpi/blob/main/SETUP_FOR_AI.md)

- **Hardware:** The owner used an RTX 3060 12 GB with tiled inference when about 2.3 GB was free; this is not a universal VRAM minimum. A Windows bundle is reported around 3.9 GB plus models; the default model is about 18 MB. System RAM and total working-space minima are not documented.
- **Software:** Windows, Python 3.13 in the setup note, CUDA-enabled PyTorch/torchvision, spandrel, PySide6 and KCC. Optional CBR input requires a separately installed UnRAR/WinRAR tool. CPU inference fallback is deliberately absent.
- **Platforms:** Windows/NVIDIA documented; Kindle Voyage and iReader SmartX/KOReader are output targets, not inference devices; macOS/Linux execution is not qualified

The Full Quality video route renders at 720p then upscales to 1080p. Small-card timings are simulations, and model/download access or rights may differ by route. No multi-user network exposure is designed into the local server.

### How it uses AI

A learned view-synthesis system models a scene through front/rear image-forming sheets, tri-probe transport and a disagreement-aware Gaussian attenuation slab. [Source](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/environment.yml)

### Introduction

A learned view-synthesis system models a scene through front/rear image-forming sheets, tri-probe transport and a disagreement-aware Gaussian attenuation slab. [Source 1](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/environment.yml)

### What it is good for

Studying compact learned scene representations and interactively comparing novel views after training on a small rendered dataset. [Source 1](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/environment.yml)

### Demo & examples

The repository includes a demo GIF and tracked small render dataset. The current sheet/transport/slab representation differs from older versions; no independent current-checkpoint benchmark or quality pass was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/environment.yml)

### Install

Create the visor-cu118 environment from environment.yml, activate it and install the checkout editable. The pinned environment uses Python 3.10, PyTorch 2.1.2/torchvision 0.16.2 and CUDA 11.8. Native tiny-cuda-nn is optional; a PyTorch fallback exists. [Source 1](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/environment.yml)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create the visor-cu118 environment from environment.yml, activate it and install the checkout editable. The pinned environment uses Python 3.10, PyTorch 2.1.2/torchvision 0.16.2 and CUDA 11.8. Native tiny-cuda-nn is optional; a PyTorch fallback exists.

```sh
conda env create -f environment.yml
```


```sh
conda activate visor-cu118
```


```sh
pip install -e .
```


```sh
bash ./run_train.sh
```


```sh
bash ./view_latest.sh
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Esemianczuk/ViSOR/blob/main/environment.yml)

1. Select the tracked renders dataset explicitly if a larger local renders1 folder exists.
2. Train and retain its configuration/checkpoint, then use the watcher to inspect progress.
3. Open the latest viewer and check interpolation, occlusion and thin structures across held-out viewpoints.

Use Windows 11, Python 3.11 and the tested package list, then prepare a Windows CUDA llama.cpp server and separate model downloads. Install Unreal 5.7, Visual Studio 2022 C++ tools and the two paid Unreal plugins. Build the supplied editor module, provide your own level/MetaHuman, configure kiosk_config.ini and start the stack.

```sh
py -3.11 -m venv .venv
```

### How it uses AI

A VST3/AU/standalone sampler generates musical loops with a local Stable Audio CPU engine or separate self-hosted GPU engines, then exposes MIDI-triggered tracks, pattern pages and effects. [Source](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.gnu.org/licenses/agpl-3.0.txt) [Source](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt)

### Introduction

A VST3/AU/standalone sampler generates musical loops with a local Stable Audio CPU engine or separate self-hosted GPU engines, then exposes MIDI-triggered tracks, pattern pages and effects. [Source 1](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.gnu.org/licenses/agpl-3.0.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt)

### What it is good for

Building a live electronic set loop by loop, with generated samples kept available for manual performance and arrangement. [Source 1](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.gnu.org/licenses/agpl-3.0.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt)

The current primary README shows the interface and a producer testimonial selected by the maintainer. It reports about 11 seconds per generation on an unspecified recent laptop CPU. This is not independently verified latency, musical quality or host compatibility. The documented CPU route selects Local Model, accepts the separate weight terms and downloads it once. The optional nine-engine server has a different checkout/setup. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.gnu.org/licenses/agpl-3.0.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/innermost47/ai-dj/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt)

1. Load the standalone/plugin and select the local model after inspecting its terms and storage needs.
2. Generate one short loop, audition it and trigger it from a track/page or MIDI input.
3. Check tempo alignment, audio-device latency and levels in the real DAW before a performance.

- **Software:** A compatible VST3/AU host or standalone release; current CPU route is Stable Audio 3 Medium. Optional server uses NVIDIA CUDA on Windows/Linux; Apple Silicon Metal server support is explicitly experimental.

## AI Influencer Studio

Video, animation & film · Avatars, digital humans & lip sync · Storyboarding, narrative & comics

The repository was created on 2026-11-03. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Producing a clearly fictional talking-character draft while retaining the character/voice pack between notebook sessions.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-03. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README shows a maintainer demo and reports roughly 1.5–2 hours for a 35–50 second first video on a T4. Its base motion is 480x832, with forward/reverse take chaining; it is not a demonstrated long continuous high-resolution performance. [Source](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Import the official notebook into Kaggle, enable its documented GPU T4 x2/Internet settings, set a private Gradio password and run the notebook. For a local Linux NVIDIA host, use the notebook setup dependencies and run studio/app.py. Export character packs before the session ends. The refreshed README says jobs run in the Kaggle background and can resume after a restart; export Full backup to include takes/videos, because session storage is temporary and this recovery was not independently tested. No independent reproduction was established. [Source](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers original code. Qwen, Wan, LatentSync, model conversions and Remotion each retain their terms; no commercial output permission is inferred from the application grant. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The Gradio share URL runs in the temporary notebook session, not as a permanent local service. Kaggle GPU allocation/quotas are not guaranteed. Base clips may smear small faces, reverse motion and chain visibly. No automatic social publication was enabled by this research. The refreshed README says jobs run in the Kaggle background and can resume after a restart; export Full backup to include takes/videos, because session storage is temporary and this recovery was not independently tested.

### How it uses AI

A notebook-hosted Gradio studio combines local Qwen scripting/voice, Wan motion, LatentSync mouth animation and Remotion/FFmpeg finishing into a vertical character video. [Source](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A notebook-hosted Gradio studio combines local Qwen scripting/voice, Wan motion, LatentSync mouth animation and Remotion/FFmpeg finishing into a vertical character video. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Producing a clearly fictional talking-character draft while retaining the character/voice pack between notebook sessions. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows a maintainer demo and reports roughly 1.5–2 hours for a 35–50 second first video on a T4. Its base motion is 480x832, with forward/reverse take chaining; it is not a demonstrated long continuous high-resolution performance. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Import the official notebook into Kaggle, enable its documented GPU T4 x2/Internet settings, set a private Gradio password and run the notebook. For a local Linux NVIDIA host, use the notebook setup dependencies and run studio/app.py. Export character packs before the session ends. The refreshed README says jobs run in the Kaggle background and can resume after a restart; export Full backup to include takes/videos, because session storage is temporary and this recovery was not independently tested. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Import the official notebook into Kaggle, enable its documented GPU T4 x2/Internet settings, set a private Gradio password and run the notebook. For a local Linux NVIDIA host, use the notebook setup dependencies and run studio/app.py. Export character packs before the session ends. The refreshed README says jobs run in the Kaggle background and can resume after a restart; export Full backup to include takes/videos, because session storage is temporary and this recovery was not independently tested.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Create a fictional character or authorized reference and prepare a close-enough face image.
2. Review the generated script and voice, then make a short base-motion take before lip sync.
3. Inspect mouth alignment, reversing/chaining artifacts, captions and audio, and export the actual finished file plus character pack. Use Full backup to carry takes/videos into a new session.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The documented cloud choice is T4 x2; local guidance is NVIDIA 16 GB+. Total model downloads, system RAM and disk minima are not fully stated. Owner timings and reported Kaggle quota are observations, not an entitlement guarantee.
- **Software:** Kaggle notebook session with enabled accelerator/Internet and required account verification; alternatively Linux/NVIDIA with 16 GB+ GPU per owner guidance. Separate model downloads and Gradio/FFmpeg/optional Remotion setup come from the notebook.
- **Platforms:** Kaggle-hosted GPU workflow; Linux NVIDIA 16 GB+ local route documented; Windows/macOS local inference is not qualified

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/ItxAsim/ai-influencer-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** MIT covers original code. Qwen, Wan, LatentSync, model conversions and Remotion each retain their terms; no commercial output permission is inferred from the application grant.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers original code. Qwen, Wan, LatentSync, model conversions and Remotion each retain their terms; no commercial output permission is inferred from the application grant.

### How it uses AI

A local audio-model discovery library learns listening preferences, searches for unfamiliar recordings and proposes explainable playlists, with optional Spotify playback and external catalog lookups. [Source](https://github.com/YatoriiK/karasu/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A local audio-model discovery library learns listening preferences, searches for unfamiliar recordings and proposes explainable playlists, with optional Spotify playback and external catalog lookups. [Source 1](https://github.com/YatoriiK/karasu/blob/main/README.md)

Use the official platform installer from cleaner.komiq.cc. First launch offers separately downloaded ONNX Runtime and model files; cloud setup is optional. Source builds need stable Rust, Node 20.19 or 22.12+ and Tauri 2 platform dependencies. [Source 1](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official platform installer from cleaner.komiq.cc. First launch offers separately downloaded ONNX Runtime and model files; cloud setup is optional. Source builds need stable Rust, Node 20.19 or 22.12+ and Tauri 2 platform dependencies.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open a small permitted chapter and run Detect before cleaning.
2. Review script selection and masks, then use the lightest suitable engine; inspect phantom marks and retained/held regions.
3. Compare the edited region and untouched source, then export PNG/TIFF/PSD or CBZ with the needed color/profile settings.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Required weights total about 317 MB; ONNX Runtime about 32 MB macOS or 200 MB Windows; optional Japanese OCR about 460 MB. Complete RAM/VRAM/storage minima are not documented. Optional FLUX sidecar needs several GB beyond this baseline.
- **Software:** macOS 11+ Apple Silicon, or Windows 10 1803+ x64; ONNX Runtime 1.28 and separate weights. Linux x64 packages/GPU path need a Vulkan loader but are physically unmeasured. Optional cloud provisioning helper is separate from local inference.
- **Platforms:** macOS Apple Silicon path measured; Windows x64 package; DirectML/WebGPU hardware performance unmeasured; Linux x64 AppImage/deb; hardware path unmeasured; Intel Macs unsupported; Linux ARM64 best-effort CPU without release package

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** GPL-3.0
- **Weights:** GPL-3.0-only covers original application code. Separately downloaded detector, LaMa, script-ID and OCR models retain their own GPL/MIT/Apache terms. Optional FLUX, Qwen, SAM text-shaped review and cloud providers need route-specific rights review.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPL-3.0-only covers original application code. Separately downloaded detector, LaMa, script-ID and OCR models retain their own GPL/MIT/Apache terms. Optional FLUX, Qwen, SAM text-shaped review and cloud providers need route-specific rights review.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Current beta status and untested optional ports do not alone classify the entire measured local route as Experimental. LaMa can leave phantom text and quality checks need tuning; optional cloud tests include fakes, limited Modal use and no Beam live run. Heavier generation may alter art. [Source 1](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/k-omiq/Bear/blob/main/README.md)

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The documentation includes native slicing/geometry benchmarks and retained receipts. Saved-file or UVtools checks are detector evidence, not successful printing, calibration or production approval. [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/docs/generation-workflows.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Create a Python environment and install the checkout editable. Install the companion Resin-Slicer separately and configure external Blender, UVtools and relevant slicer paths. FDM uses an installed OrcaSlicer CLI; imported Bambu presets require the corresponding Bambu Studio route. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/docs/generation-workflows.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers original Agent-Slicer code. Companion slicers, Blender, UVtools, vendor profiles and model providers keep their own licenses; no reference slicer code is copied into this package. Hosted generation and output permissions are separate. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/docs/generation-workflows.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Orientation and most geometry planning are deterministic, not learned optimizers. Local high-quality neural image-to-3D inference is not implemented on the documented 4 GB laptop. Verified FDM scope is single-plate/single-filament P2S profiles; other printers and physical outcomes remain outside those checks.

### How it uses AI

An agent-first CLI/SDK accepts typed fabrication projects and offers gated Meshy/Tripo concept generation before deterministic geometry preparation, slicing and evidence-bearing validation. [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/docs/generation-workflows.md)

### Introduction

Bringing AI-generated miniature/sculpture candidates into a reviewable resin/FDM preparation workflow with explicit dimensions and retained rejected artifacts. Create a Python environment and install the checkout editable. Install the companion Resin-Slicer separately and configure external Blender, UVtools and relevant slicer paths. FDM uses an installed OrcaSlicer CLI; imported Bambu presets require the corresponding Bambu Studio route. [Source 1](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/docs/generation-workflows.md)

```sh
python -m venv .venv

```

3. Run plan, preparation, slice and saved-file validation, then inspect reports and physically calibrate before printing.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rubenschmeitz/AgentSlicer/blob/main/docs/generation-workflows.md)

- **Platforms:** Standalone Windows setup documented; Native Linux/WSL validation guidance; A complete macOS or cross-printer acceptance matrix is not established

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Linear merging is not orthogonal activation control; composition remains seed-sensitive. Strength clamps each component, so 1.25 does not uniformly add 25 percent. Saved diffusion weights exclude the encoder/VAE.

### How it uses AI

A ComfyUI node interpolates MOSS Base/SFT model components, exposing presets or 51 component weights before neural image inference. [Source](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A ComfyUI node interpolates MOSS Base/SFT model components, exposing presets or 51 component weights before neural image inference. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Same-seed comparisons of composition preservation, typography and material-detail tradeoffs in MOSS images. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

Owner comparison sheets show Base, SFT and three presets, including portrait strengths. Layer guidance is empirical; no broad independent quality validation is established. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Clone the repository into ComfyUI/custom_nodes, restart, supply separately downloaded Base/SFT, text encoder and VAE, then import the example workflow. Install the separate prompt enhancer only if used, or bypass it. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the repository into ComfyUI/custom_nodes, restart, supply separately downloaded Base/SFT, text encoder and VAE, then import the example workflow. Install the separate prompt enhancer only if used, or bypass it.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets.git
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

1. Load Base and SFT, connect the merge output into the native MOSS sampling chain.
2. Begin with Balanced/strength 1 and compare identical prompts/seeds across presets.
3. Inspect multiple seeds; save the merged model through a separate ModelSave branch if desired.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Owner test uses RTX 5090; a minimum VRAM/RAM or total storage requirement is not published.
- **Software:** ComfyUI 0.42.0+ recommended, native MOSS loader/sampling chain; no additional plugin-specific Python dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Tested Windows, ComfyUI 0.42.0 and RTX 5090; other platforms not established by that test

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT node code; Base/SFT, encoder, VAE and any LoRAs retain separate weight terms and downloads.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT node code; Base/SFT, encoder, VAE and any LoRAs retain separate weight terms and downloads.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Linear merging is not orthogonal activation control; composition remains seed-sensitive. Strength clamps each component, so 1.25 does not uniformly add 25 percent. Saved diffusion weights exclude the encoder/VAE. [Source 1](https://github.com/slmonker/ComfyUI-MOSS-Merge-Presets/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

### Introduction

A ComfyUI integration applies a MOSS edit adapter through native model patching, conditioning the neural sampler on a source image and optional ordered references. [Source 1](https://github.com/Chenkin-x/ComfyUI-Moss-Image-Edit/blob/main/README.md)

Claims of better physical consistency are author research results, not independently verified production reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/TencentARC/Looking-Ahead/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/TencentARC/Looking-Ahead/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

### Install

On the documented Linux/NVIDIA environment use Python 3.12 with uv sync or the pinned conda/pip route, download the corresponding upstream Wan components and FCP checkpoint, then run the matching single-GPU image/video example from the repository root. [Source 1](https://github.com/TencentARC/Looking-Ahead/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/TencentARC/Looking-Ahead/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. On the documented Linux/NVIDIA environment use Python 3.12 with uv sync or the pinned conda/pip route, download the corresponding upstream Wan components and FCP checkpoint, then run the matching single-GPU image/video example from the repository root.

```sh
uv sync --python 3.12
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/TencentARC/Looking-Ahead/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/TencentARC/Looking-Ahead/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

1. Select one matching base/FCP configuration and download its encoder/VAE/checkpoint files.
2. Use the supplied sailboat or milk-pouring example and its prompt for a short five-second generation.
3. Compare with the corresponding baseline, inspecting artifacts and physical consistency across several inputs.

- **Software:** Linux, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.10.0+cu128, CUDA 12.8, compatible NVIDIA driver, C++ compiler and FFmpeg.
- **Platforms:** Linux/NVIDIA tested; Windows/macOS support not documented in the reviewed setup

Claude agents write scripts, plan references and author vector assets; a pipeline coordinates optional paid voice/image/video generation and Remotion rendering. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Human-approved multilingual explainer drafts with reusable characters, SVG/3D-look motion modes and an inspectable cost ledger. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README describes style samples, local render-demo and approval panels. Local no-API rendering tests only the renderer, while claimed reality/physics gates are model checks rather than independently verified truth or physical simulation. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Node 24+, pnpm 11, FFmpeg and a signed-in Claude installation/API. Install dependencies, configure chosen providers and first run the no-API render demo. Open the Studio on port 3100, approve script and concepts before media production. Database infrastructure is optional. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Node 24+, pnpm 11, FFmpeg and a signed-in Claude installation/API. Install dependencies, configure chosen providers and first run the no-API render demo. Open the Studio on port 3100, approve script and concepts before media production. Database infrastructure is optional.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm ys render-demo
```


```sh
pnpm studio
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

1. Create a short vector-mode episode and inspect the cost estimate.
2. Approve/edit script and the reference-linked character/concept gates.
3. Review draft frames and narration, then download the final video/SRT and package; agent costs are outside the media cap.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node 24+, pnpm 11, FFmpeg; optional Manim 0.22/LaTeX. Remotion/Chrome rendering with GPU or software path. Claude and selected provider access are separate.
- **Platforms:** Windows optional-tool instructions supplied; Node-based setup described; a complete tested OS matrix is not published

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT project code; Remotion company-license thresholds apply separately, as do fal/Google/ElevenLabs, Wikimedia reference rights and generated-media terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project code; Remotion company-license thresholds apply separately, as do fal/Google/ElevenLabs, Wikimedia reference rights and generated-media terms.
- **Cost:** Local render plus separately billed agents and chosen media services; exact current charges depend on providers. RAM/VRAM/total disk minima are not documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The per-episode media cap excludes Claude agent calls. Optional Gemini Omni video has not been verified with a real run. Automatic drawing gates do not establish historical accuracy or aesthetically successful animation. [Source 1](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/ersinkoc/AI-Video-Factory/blob/main/README.md)

## AI Video Marker

The repository was created on 2026-10-25. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: A scoped, resumable pixel-film authoring workflow with a representative-shot review before extending the full story.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-03. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/Lanqiao-Luca/ai-video-marker/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The source links an approximately 30-second comic example and a 44-second pixel film. Its default 36-second harbor example is a procedural renderer, not a built-in AI generator. These are owner production records; Scout did not play or reproduce them. [Source](https://github.com/Lanqiao-Luca/ai-video-marker/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.9+ in a virtual environment with the repository requirements. Its default harbor renderer needs no API, Node or private source assets. For a new story, open the project in a capable AI coding host, first plan/adapt a representative segment and preserve project records; initializing a project alone produces no film. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Lanqiao-Luca/ai-video-marker/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT source, original music and explicitly identified original media; module-level third-party and user-provided asset rights remain separate. External generation/agent and editor terms are not included. Supplied procedural scenes require local compute only; external AI authoring, new imagery, voice or commercial editors may incur separate charges. [Source](https://github.com/Lanqiao-Luca/ai-video-marker/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Nine documented routes include three candidates; several are methods/blank templates rather than completed automatic production tools. There is no autonomous state scheduler, generic story generator or bundled Blender project. The comic example omits its original generated images/editor project. The guide is scoped to pixel authoring, not all nine routes.

### How it uses AI

An external AI creative agent uses staged story/art/motion/audio workflows and local Python rendering/inspection tools to author limited-animation and pixel short films. [Source](https://github.com/Lanqiao-Luca/ai-video-marker/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Use Python 3.9+ in a virtual environment with the repository requirements. Its default harbor renderer needs no API, Node or private source assets. ### Install

Use Node 20+, install npm dependencies, copy the example environment locally and supply Anthropic plus any chosen media-provider keys. Start development on localhost:3000. Without Supabase, work stays in the browser; accounts/cloud saves require the supplied schema, explicitly created users and a configured admin allowlist. 2. Use Node 20+, install npm dependencies, copy the example environment locally and supply Anthropic plus any chosen media-provider keys. Start development on localhost:3000. Without Supabase, work stays in the browser; accounts/cloud saves require the supplied schema, explicitly created users and a configured admin allowlist.

```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RaphaelRangel-AI/Slateboard/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/raphaelrangel-ai/slateboard/blob/main/NOTICE)

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/RaphaelRangel-AI/Slateboard/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/raphaelrangel-ai/slateboard/blob/main/NOTICE)

### Limitations

About 85–90 percent of the original plan is reported complete. The working beta and unfinished broader plan alone do not establish that the scoped core workflow is unstable; quality remains unverified.
